Use before accepting a fix round, before a version bump or publication, when green gates are the only evidence a change works, when a defect has recurred across rounds, or whenever a review would otherwise read a diff and agree with it.\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Falsify\r\n\r\nGreen gates prove a suite ran. They prove nothing about the claim the work makes. This skill is for\r\nthe moment when everything passes and the work is still not known to be right.\r\n\r\n## Load authority\r\n\r\n1. `AGENTS.md`.\r\n2. `.claude/rules/quality.md`, especially **Falsification** �?\" the laws governing auditor behaviour.\r\n   This skill prescribes the round; that section prescribes the conduct inside it. Do not restate it.\r\n3. The governing guide/spec for the subject.\r\n4. `references/brief.md` before writing a brief; `references/reconcile.md` before ruling.\r\n\r\n## When a round is warranted\r\n\r\nRun one when the work is finished and the evidence is weak in a specific way:\r\n\r\n- gates are green and nothing has attacked the claim;\r\n- a fix round is about to be accepted, especially one whose author reported success;\r\n- a version is about to be bumped, packed, or published;\r\n- the same defect class has now appeared in more than one round;\r\n- a self-declared \"sound, unchanged, no fix needed\" verdict is load-bearing.\r\n\r\nDo not run one for a typo, a mechanical rename, or work whose failure would be immediately visible.\r\n\r\nA round also needs something new to attack. When the previous round's claims all held, nothing has\r\nbeen added or repaired since, and the only motive is that an auditor could still imagine an attack,\r\nthere is no subject �?\" closing is the correct action and the next subject is the deliverable.\r\n\r\nWhen the same class has recurred along one seam to the budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\r\nA\u0015 Rounds and verdicts, the next move is that law's strategy switch �?\" the breadth sweep of the\r\nstream, the ruling, or the dropped moving-target claim �?\" rather than another successor round.\r\n\r\n## Write the brief\r\n\r\nThe brief is the instrument. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\r\nit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\r\n\r\n- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\r\n  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\r\n- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\r\n  politeness.\r\n- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\r\n\r\nThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's �?\" read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\r\nskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\r\n\r\n## Evidence, by subject type\r\n\r\nThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\r\ndiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\r\n\r\n| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\r\n| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\r\n| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\r\n\r\n**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\r\nalready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\r\nthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\r\nnobody claimed.\r\n\r\n## Run the round\r\n\r\n- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\r\n  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\r\n  looking like authority �?\" and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\r\n  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\r\n  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure. Where the\r\n  executor's tree carries a superseded vendored copy of an authority, take the stale-authority\r\n  branch in `references/brief.md` A\u0015 \"What not to put in a brief\".\r\n- Run the **adversarial pass** on one identical brief: a subjective lane and an objective\r\n  lane, each a fresh subagent with a clean context, blind to each other. Reconcile them yourself.\r\n  `.agents/orchestration.md` owns lane definitions, engine assignment, and what happens when an\r\n  engine is dark; do not restate them here.\r\n- `.agents/orchestration.md` A\u0015 Execution loop owns which lanes a round runs and the deviation a\r\n  short round records; A\u0015 Engine assignment owns the substitution.\r\n- **Pair every finder with an independent refuter when the round fans out past the subjective and\r\n  objective lanes.** The\r\n  refuter receives one slice's findings, never that finder's work, and is briefed to BREAK them\r\n  rather than to re-audit the subject. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\r\n  refuted when uncertain.\r\n- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\r\n  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\r\n  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\r\n  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong �?\" then CONFIRM with the correction.\r\n- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\r\n  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\r\n- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\r\n  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict �?\" it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\r\n- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\r\n  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\r\n  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\r\n  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\r\n  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\r\n  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\r\n  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\r\n  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\r\n  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\r\n  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\r\n- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\r\n  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\r\n  round. **This binds\r\n  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\r\n  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\r\n  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\r\n- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\r\n  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\r\n  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\r\n  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\r\n- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\r\n- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\r\n  tools.\r\n- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\r\n  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\r\n  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\r\n- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\r\n  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\r\n  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\r\n- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised �?\" by\r\n  anyone, including the auditor �?\" once it has been returned.\r\n\r\n## Verdict shape\r\n\r\nEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\r\ncomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\r\n\r\n1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\r\n\r\n   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\r\n   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\r\n   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires �?\" note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\r\n   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it; a claim whose only evidence is the writer's own report takes this value                                                                 |\r\n   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\r\n\r\n   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\r\n   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\r\n   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the auditing lanes' own token; it is kept, not re-invented.\r\n\r\n2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\r\n\r\n3. **Attacked and held**: the claims this round attacked and could not break, each with the attack\r\n   that failed, and the adjacent behaviour that looks like the defect and is correct. This is where\r\n   the next round reads what has already been tried, so a claim listed here without its attack is\r\n   worth nothing to it. A claim's own `CONFIRMED` line already carries the evidence that convinced\r\n   the auditor, so list here only the attacks no verdict line carries and the adjacent behaviour.\r\n\r\n4. **One terminal line**, and only one:\r\n\r\n   ```text\r\n   VERDICT: PASS\r\n   VERDICT: FAIL <claim numbers>; outside the claims: <finding ids>\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n   The claim numbers are every claim that is not `CONFIRMED`. Write `none` in a slot the round\r\n   leaves empty, so a `FAIL` driven only by a finding outside the claims still names both.\r\n\r\n   **`PASS` requires all of this**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\r\n   forces `FAIL` however the numbered claims landed �?\" otherwise a round can report a real\r\n   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\r\n\r\nNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\r\n\r\n## When a round comes back all-confirmed\r\n\r\nThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\r\nclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\r\n\r\n## Reconcile and rule\r\n\r\nFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\r\n\r\n- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\r\n  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\r\n- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\r\n  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\r\n  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\r\n  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\r\n- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\r\n  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\r\n  the subject's claims �?\" demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\r\n  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\r\n- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\r\n  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\r\n- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\r\n  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\r\n- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\r\n  in the fix brief as a constraint.\r\n- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\r\n\r\n## Accept, or run it again\r\n\r\nThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** �?\"\r\nevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\r\nsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. The alternative is a consumer finding it\r\nafter publication, when the version number is already spent. But an unsubstantiated attack is not a\r\nfinding, and the supply of imaginable ones never runs out: a round is not re-run because an auditor\r\ncan still think of one. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\r\n`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\r\n`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam �?\" after\r\nenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\r\n\r\nWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\r\nbrief is the successor of this one.\r\n# Developer-facing writing rules\r\n\r\nThese rules govern prose a developer reads, including chat replies, reports, guides, README files,\r\nand commit messages.\r\n`AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing governs prose everywhere, and this file does not restate it; this file adds\r\nonly what a developer audience decides. An instruction file follows `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Instruction files\r\nfirst, and these rules wherever a rule here names no different form for it.\r\n\r\n## Voice and actor\r\n\r\n- Write `must` for a requirement, `can` for an option or an ability, and `might` for a possibility.\r\n  A recommendation takes the imperative, the same form `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing fixes for instructions.\r\n  Never write `should`, and never soften a recommendation into `We recommend`.\r\n- Address the developer as `you`. Name the software component that acts, and make it the subject of\r\n  the sentence. Reserve `user` for someone using software the developer builds. Never write `we`,\r\n  `our`, or `let's` about agent work.\r\n- Give software no human faculties. A component reports, returns, detects, or refuses; it never\r\n  knows, thinks, wants, or sees.\r\n- Use the passive voice only where naming the actor adds blame and nothing else, such as a defect\r\n  count. Name the actor wherever the reader needs it.\r\n- Use negative contractions (`don't`, `isn't`, `can't`) in a reply or a guide. Do not use them in an\r\n  instruction file.\r\n\r\n## Sentence and paragraph order\r\n\r\n- Put the condition, the goal, or the location before the instruction, so a reader the instruction\r\n  does not apply to stops at the first clause. Put the result or the reason after it. This governs a\r\n  sentence, not a document: a report still opens with its finding.\r\n- Put the key point in the first sentence of every paragraph and every list item, not only in the\r\n  opening of the reply or the section.\r\n- Keep the helper words `that`, `then`, `of`, `a`, and `the`. Do not drop one for brevity.\r\n- Name the noun after `this`, `these`, or `it` wherever the reader could attach the pronoun to\r\n  another referent.\r\n- State what the reader can do. Do not write a double negative.\r\n\r\n## Claims and time\r\n\r\n- Claim only what the reader can check. Never write `ensure`, `guarantee`, a superlative, or an\r\n  effort adjective as a claim about behavior, and cite the run behind every number. `ensure`\r\n  addressed to an executor as a directive is outside this ban.\r\n- Write the present tense for what exists. Do not write `currently`, `now`, `new`, `latest`, or\r\n  `soon`; where time matters, give the version or the date.\r\n- Recommend one path, the shortest one you proved. Rule on every option you list, and drop an option\r\n  equivalent to the one you recommend.\r\n\r\n## Code tokens, references, and links\r\n\r\n- Put a code token in backticks and follow it with a noun: the `parse` method, the `vite.config.ts`\r\n  file, the `--check` flag. Never inflect, pluralize, or possessivize a code token, and never use one\r\n  as an English verb.\r\n- Point to other material with `preceding`, `following`, `earlier`, or `later`, never with `above` or\r\n  `below`. Use `earlier` and `later` for a version range too.\r\n- Write link text as the destination's title or a descriptive phrase, introduced by `see`. Never\r\n  write `here`, `this document`, or a bare URL in prose.\r\n- Paraphrase third-party content and link its source; never paste it into prose you author. A\r\n  vendored mirror is fetched bytes rather than authored prose, and `.claude/rules/documentation.md`\r\n  governs it.\r\n\r\n## Structure\r\n\r\n- Keep a required fact in the main flow. A note or a notice carries only what the reader can skip,\r\n  never a prerequisite, a step, or a warning the task depends on.\r\n- In a reply or a guide, introduce every list, table, and code fence with a complete sentence naming\r\n  what follows; a rule file's list sits bare under its heading. Number a list only where order or\r\n  rank matters. Use a table only for rows with comparable fields.\r\n- Write a heading in sentence case, verb first for a task and a noun phrase for a concept. Identity\r\n  numbering �?\" a claim, an audit verdict, a plan unit �?\" is data rather than a heading style, and\r\n  stays.\r\n\r\n## Examples, numbers, and abbreviations\r\n\r\n- Build an example from fictional data with descriptive names and no personally identifiable\r\n  information. Write a placeholder in `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` and explain it on first use. Never write\r\n  `foo`, `bar`, or `baz`.\r\n- Expand an abbreviation the reader may not know on first use, with the short form in parentheses.\r\n  Skip the expansion for one this audience reads daily, such as `API`, `CLI`, `JSON`, `URL`, or a\r\n  file format.\r\n- Write a numeral for a technical quantity, a version, or a measurement. Write a date as\r\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` in evidence, commit messages, and reports. See `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing for what\r\n  separates a value from a count.\r\n- Use the serial comma. Mark omitted code with a comment in the sample's language, never with `...`.\r\n\r\n## Substitutions\r\n\r\nReplace each term in this table with its replacement. A literal code identifier is data, and so is\r\na sample string inside a code fence or a test fixture: quote each as itself, exempt from every row.\r\n\r\n| Term                     | Replacement                               |\r\n| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |\r\n| `should`                 | `must`, `can`, `might`, or the imperative |\r\n| `simply`, `easy`, `just` | Delete                                    |\r\n| `currently`, `now`       | Delete, or give the date                  |\r\n| `new`, `latest`          | Delete, or give the version               |\r\n| `utilize`, `leverage`    | `use`                                     |\r\n| `via`                    | `through`, `by using`                     |\r\n| `in order to`            | `to`                                      |\r\n| `e.g.`, `i.e.`           | `for example`, `that is`                  |\r\n| `etc.`                   | Bound the list, or recast the sentence    |\r\n| `performant`, `robust`   | The measured property                     |\r\n| `allows you to`          | `lets you`                                |\r\n| `and/or`                 | `and`, `or`, or `both`                    |\r\n| `since` (causal)         | `because`                                 |\r\n| `once` (temporal)        | `after`                                   |\r\n| `please`                 | Delete                                    |\r\n| `sanity check`           | `quick check`                             |\r\n| `dummy`                  | `placeholder`                             |\r\n| `blacklist`, `whitelist` | `denylist`, `allowlist`                   |\r\n| `master`, `slave`        | `primary`, `replica`                      |\r\n\r\n- `policy/no-banned-term` reads every comment and the prose sweep in `tests/setupPolicy.ts` reads\r\n  every authored Markdown file, and each matches the rows this table bans unconditionally. The rule\r\n  and the sweep leave `now`, `new`, `latest`, `once`, `since`, and `master` unmatched because those\r\n  rows carry a permitted sense, so rule a hit in one of those rows yourself.\r\n- Sweep case-insensitively and across inflections when checking prose against the preceding table. A\r\n  pattern for `easy` reaches neither `Easy` nor `easier`, and a temporal `once` most often appears\r\n  as a sentence-initial `Once`.\r\n- Rule every hit by the sense its row bans, not by the match. `once` counts as often as it means\r\n  `after`, and `new` names a value as often as it dates one. Record a hit in a permitted sense as\r\n  permitted rather than dropping it.\r\n- Name the pattern and the paths behind every sweep result, including a clean one. A result naming\r\n  neither reports on the population its pattern admitted rather than on the population it was drawn\r\n  from.\r\n- Write singular `they` for a person of unstated gender.\r\n- This table carries no row for `execute`, `abort`, `kill`, `terminate`, or `run`:\r\n  `.claude/rules/names.md` A\u0015 Fixed lifecycle vocabulary owns those words, and no row here overrides\r\n  it.\r\n\r\n## Not adopted\r\n\r\n- Do not add conversational personality, warmth, or memorability to a reply; the prose stays plain.\r\n- Do not import a license to break these rules where a departure reads better; an exception exists\r\n  only where a rule states it.\r\n- `We recommend` as a recommendation form �?\" refused in A\u0015 Voice and actor.\r\n- Do not apply a source guide's sample indentation or line-length chrome; the repository formatter\r\n  and `.claude/rules/typescript.md` decide code presentation.\r\n- Do not write an unspaced em dash; this project spaces it.\r\n- Do not cap a sentence at a word count; `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing owns sentence length.\r\n- Do not treat an external word list as vocabulary law; the preceding substitution table is the\r\n  adopted set.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - '**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts,vue}'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# TypeScript rules\r\n\r\nThe non-negotiables and design laws in `AGENTS.md` apply without exception and are not restated here. This file adds only what TypeScript itself decides.\r\n\r\n## Syntax and imports\r\n\r\n- Tabs for indentation.\r\n- No semicolons unless ASI requires one.\r\n- Single quotes.\r\n- Named exports only, except framework-required defaults.\r\n- ESM imports use explicit `.js` extensions for local TypeScript modules.\r\n- Place `import type` declarations before value imports.\r\n- Do not place blank lines between consecutive imports of the same kind.\r\n- Narrow an accepted `unknown` with a total guard rather than a conditional access.\r\n- A parameter property is a constructor parameter carrying any accessibility or `readonly` modifier.\r\n  The ban reaches every form, `readonly` alone included; declare the `#` field and assign it in the\r\n  constructor body.\r\n\r\n## Types\r\n\r\n- Put every reusable or public interface/type alias in the nearest authoritative `*/types.ts`.\r\n- Public collection properties and return types use `readonly T[]`, `ReadonlyMap<K, V>`, or `ReadonlySet<T>`.\r\n- Optional state is `T | undefined`; an optional lookup failure returns `undefined`.\r\n- `as const` annotates a literal with its own type and never overrides the checker, so the assertion\r\n  ban does not reach it. Use it to derive a literal union from a value and to fix a tuple's arity and\r\n  element types. Do not write it on a value whose contract is already declared; annotate the\r\n  declaration instead. A class field that holds one literal keeps `as const`\r\n  (`readonly code = 'ABORT' as const`): the vendored lint gate's `prefer-as-const` rule refuses the\r\n  annotated form, and a field whose type is a union of literals is annotated as the preceding\r\n  sentence states.\r\n\r\n## Immutability\r\n\r\n- Never mutate caller-owned inputs.\r\n- Use copy-on-write for internal state.\r\n- Return copies or readonly views; never leak a mutable internal reference.\r\n- Compute derived facts instead of persisting duplicate state.\r\n\r\n## Errors and outcomes\r\n\r\n| Condition                            | Required strategy                           |\r\n| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Programmer error or invalid argument | Throw an `AppError`                         |\r\n| I/O/network/external operation       | Return `Result<T, E>` or throw consistently |\r\n| Optional missing lookup              | Return `undefined`                          |\r\n| Invalid input inside a guard         | Return `false`; never throw                 |\r\n\r\nUse the existing outcome contract from a declared dependency or the owning environment; do not redeclare it at each call site. When no such contract exists and adding a dependency is not authorized, define this once in the owning `types.ts`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface Success<T> {\r\n\treadonly success: true\r\n\treadonly value: T\r\n}\r\n\r\ninterface Failure<E> {\r\n\treadonly success: false\r\n\treadonly error: E\r\n}\r\n\r\ntype Result<T, E = Error> = Success<T> | Failure<E>\r\n```\r\n\r\n- When `@orkestrel/contract` is declared, inspect and use its exact installed `Result`, construction/narrowing helpers, and `attempt` behavior instead of duplicating them.\r\n- Otherwise construct and narrow through the owning environment's centralized helpers.\r\n- Error classes expose a machine-readable `code` and optional `context`.\r\n- Every public error class ships with a guard such as `isAppError` for safe `catch` narrowing.\r\n\r\n## Comments and API documentation\r\n\r\n- Comments explain why, never restate what self-explanatory code does. The Writing rules in `AGENTS.md` and `.claude/rules/writing.md` govern their prose.\r\n- Every public export has complete TSDoc: description, `@param`, `@returns`, and `@example` where applicable.\r\n- The first sentence states what the symbol does in the third person with an `-s` verb �?\" `Creates`,\r\n  `Returns`, `Checks whether` �?\" and never repeats the symbol's name.\r\n- `policy/no-malformed-summary` reads that first sentence over every doc block a top-level export\r\n  declaration follows, and refuses an opening word that is not a third-person `-s` verb and a\r\n  sentence naming the declared symbol. A word ending in `s` that the rule's stop set does not name\r\n  passes whether or not it is a verb �?\" a plural noun such as `Files` included �?\" so read the sentence\r\n  in review as well.\r\n- Describe a boolean parameter as \"If `true`, �?�; if `false`, �?�\", and a boolean return as\r\n  \"True if �?�; false otherwise\".\r\n- Write a default as \"Default: �?�\" and a thrown error as \"Thrown when �?�\".\r\n- State a prerequisite and the failure behavior wherever the symbol has either.\r\n- `@deprecated` names the replacement first, then the reason.\r\n- Document an options object as one `@param`; describe its short fields under `@remarks`.\r\n- Private methods and overload-specific notes use single-line `//` comments, not public TSDoc.\r\n- Do not document speculative future product behavior unless requested.\r\n- Never use `@internal` to hide a method; make it `#` private.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/*.{ts,vue}'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Architecture and placement rules\r\n\r\n## Centralized-file pattern\r\n\r\n| Content                   | Sole location                                                |\r\n| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| Interfaces/types          | `*/types.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Constants/data            | `*/constants.ts`                                             |\r\n| Template definitions      | `*/templates.ts`                                             |\r\n| Pure helpers              | `*/helpers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Guards                    | `*/validators.ts`                                            |\r\n| Guard combinators         | `*/combinators.ts`                                           |\r\n| Owned snapshots           | `*/cloners.ts`                                               |\r\n| Coercers                  | `*/parsers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Shape values              | `*/shapers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Value inferers            | `*/inferers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Shape/algorithm compilers | `*/middlewares.ts`                                           |\r\n| Request handlers          | `*/handlers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Route tables              | `*/schemas.ts`                                               |\r\n| Compiled contracts        | `*/contracts.ts`                                             |\r\n| Relations                 | `*/relations.ts`                                             |\r\n| Error classes/guards      | `*/errors.ts`                                                |\r\n| Public exports            | `*/index.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Implementations           | `*/[domain]/[Entity].ts` �?\" one class per file                |\r\n| Function modules          | a imports and exactly one class implementation with `#` fields.\r\n- It contains no module-scope interface, type, constant, or free function�?\"even when private to that file.\r\n- A function module contains imports and exactly one exported function named for its file. Everything else extracts by kind exactly as it does from a class module.\r\n- Extract local declarations by kind. �?oOnly used here�?? and �?onot exported�?? are not exemptions.\r\n- Every declaration in a centralized file is exported. Fold away a trivial single-use declaration or export/test it; never leave it hidden.\r\n- The only permitted non-exported module-scope declarations are in a runtime entrypoint that must be self-contained and cannot import siblings, such as raw source loaded in a worker. Explain that necessity in a comment.\r\n- A runtime entry�?\"`src/bin/main.ts`, `app/browser/main.ts`, `app/server/main.ts`�?\"is a fixed name, not a centralized kind file. Both the data rule and the function rule reach it, so it declares no module-scope constant and no module-scope function: it imports what it needs and runs. The preceding self-contained exception covers only an entrypoint that cannot import siblings.\r\n- Perform a cleanup pass after implementation: no stray implementation-file declarations, non-exported/wrong-kind centralized declarations, prohibited nested declarations, duplicate implementations, compatibility aliases, superfluous wrappers, stale imports/barrel rows, or untested extracted functions.\r\n\r\n## Kind purity\r\n\r\n- Each centralized file contains only its named kind.\r\n- Module-scope constants live only in `constants.ts`, use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE, and freeze object/array data with `Object.freeze`.\r\n- A camelCase namespace containing functions is helper behavior, not constant data; place it in `helpers.ts`.\r\n- `helpers.ts` is exported reusable infrastructure. A reusable regex, parser, header flattener, signer, or similar fragment has one implementation used by source, tests, and fixtures.\r\n- A would-be helper has these outcomes:\r\n  - trivial and genuinely single-use �+' fold into its caller;\r\n  - non-trivial or reusable �+' extract, export, unit-test, and route every duplicate through it.\r\n- `factories.ts`, `compilers.ts`, and `parsers.ts` are centralized files, not hiding places. Factory glue extracts to `helpers.ts`; pure compiler/parser recursion remains exported in its own kind file.\r\n- Every exported function in `parsers.ts` is named `parse*`. Every exported function in\r\n  `factories.ts` is named `create*`.\r\n- Those name forms are one-directional. A name does not place a function: `createWriteDirectory`\r\n  creates a directory rather than an entity and `isVacant` is a predicate rather than a `Guard<T>`,\r\n  so both stay in `helpers.ts`. Placement follows what the function is; the name form follows\r\n  placement.\r\n- Repair a violation of those forms by deciding what the function **is** first, then moving or\r\n  renaming to match. Both repairs exist and picking the wrong one does real damage.\r\n  - Wrong file, right name �+' **move it**. A `scan*` in `parsers.ts` is a pure lexical leaf that\r\n    belongs in `helpers.ts`. The barrel star-exports both, so the move leaves the published surface\r\n    identical.\r\n  - Right file, wrong name �+' **rename it in place**. An entity is a class instance whose methods\r\n    drive its own state, as opposed to a plain value that carries data and no behaviour. A function\r\n    returning a live entity is an entity factory and belongs in `factories.ts` whatever it is\r\n    called, so `restoreThing` there is misnamed, not misplaced. Renaming moves the published\r\n    surface and earns a version bump; that cost is the correct one to pay, and it is smaller than\r\n    the alternative.\r\n  - Never let the name choose. Relocating a correctly-placed function to escape a rename drags its\r\n    dependencies with it �?\" an entity factory moved into `helpers.ts` makes that file import an\r\n    implementation class, and a leaf file that imports a class stops being a leaf for every module\r\n    beneath it.\r\n- Keep the leaf pair class-free. `helpers.ts` and `validators.ts` sit at the bottom of a module's\r\n  graph: they import types, constants, errors, and each other, and they import no implementation\r\n  class. Every file that constructs or drives a class �?\" `cloners.ts`, `compilers.ts`, `factories.ts`,\r\n  `shapers.ts` �?\" sits above them, consumes them, and is never consumed by them. One cycle between\r\n  the leaves is the shape this produces and is acceptable; an edge running downward from a\r\n  class-importing file into the leaf pair is not.\r\n- `templates.ts` and `contracts.ts` hold data only �?\" shipped template definitions and compiled\r\n  contracts. A function that builds either belongs in the kind file for what it builds.\r\n- `handlers.ts` holds request handlers, which are functions. `routes.ts` holds data only: a route is\r\n  `{ method, path, handler }`, so the table maps each path to a handler declared in `handlers.ts`\r\n  and referenced by name, never to a function expression written in place. Neither file mixes kinds and the\r\n  route set stays readable as data.\r\n\r\n### What the policy instruments prove\r\n\r\nThe `policy` Oxlint plugin (`configs/policy.ts`) enforces syntactic placement: a declaration of a\r\ngiven syntactic kind appears only in a file permitted to hold that kind. The fleet policy sweep\r\n(`tests/policy.test.ts`, `tests/setupPolicy.ts`) enforces what a path or a text reading decides.\r\nThe plugin reads declaration syntax and file name; the sweep reads paths and workspace text;\r\nneither reads meaning.\r\n\r\n- The plugin proves that a module function sits in a function-kind file, that module data sits in a\r\n  data-kind file, that every centralized declaration is exported, that a class sits in its matching\r\n  implementation or errors file, and that `constants.ts` declares only UPPER_SNAKE_CASE consts with\r\n  no bare collection literal.\r\n- The sweep proves that no source, test, config, or script file carries an `eslint-disable` or\r\n  `oxlint-disable` directive.\r\n- The sweep proves that every `.claude/rules/*.md` file has a rule-map row in `AGENTS.md` and that\r\n  every row resolves to a file.\r\n- The sweep proves the host portability rules that are path- or text-shaped over the population\r\n  `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB` names: no path segment carries a Windows reserved device name, a\r\n  character Windows refuses, a trailing dot or space, or a sibling differing only by case; and no\r\n  `package.json` script names a `.sh` file.\r\n- The plugin proves, over the population `POLICY_ENDING_GLOBS` names, that no source trims a\r\n  payload before splitting it on `'\\n'`, reads `os.EOL`, or imports `EOL` from `node:os`.\r\n## Environment/module placement\r\n\r\n- Shared cross-environment logic belongs in the central core/shared layer. Other environments import core; core imports neither browser nor server.\r\n- An environment with multiple modules keeps cross-module types/helpers at the environment root and module-specific declarations inside the module.\r\n- Promote a declaration to the environment root only when at least two modules use it.\r\n- The environment barrel re-exports root files and module barrels.\r\n- Cover cross-cutting environment-root helpers with behavioral tests; each module API receives its own guide parity.\r\n\r\n### Entity subfolders\r\n\r\n- When one entity grows a family (entity + manager or sibling implementations), nest only its class files in a lowercase plural folder.\r\n- Keep its interfaces in module-root `types.ts`, factories in module-root `factories.ts`, and exports in the module barrel.\r\n- Entity subfolders never grow their own centralized files.\r\n\r\n### Kind or folder\r\n\r\n- A word is either a centralized kind or a domain folder, never both.\r\n- A folder named for a centralized kind�?\"`helpers/`, `validators/`, `handlers/`�?\"is that kind's file, not a folder.\r\n- `FUNCTION_DOMAIN_FOLDERS` in the fleet-canon register (`configs/policy.ts`) registers the\r\n  folder paths whose direct modules are function modules. Registration makes a path eligible and\r\n  fixes the declaration shape checked there; it judges nothing about what a module does.\r\n- Never infer a function domain from a folder's name; a camelCase module inside an unregistered\r\n  folder is misplaced.\r\n- Register a folder only when its direct modules are camelCase single-function `.ts` modules. A\r\n  folder of PascalCase single-file components, or of entity classes, never qualifies.\r\n- Request a new domain through a fleet-canon change. There is no workspace-local registration path.\r\n\r\n### Extension categories\r\n\r\n- A designed extension point�?\"drivers, stores, transports�?\"gets its category folder even with one concrete class.\r\n- This applies both to consumer implementations of another domain's contract and default implementations beside the defining contract.\r\n- The contract remains in module-root `types.ts`; only concrete classes nest.\r\n- Nest because the category is a designed growth seam, not because a class name happens to end in `Store` or `Driver`.\r\n- Co-equal native-object wrappers that are core primitives stay flat.\r\n\r\n### Stores\r\n\r\nChoose the access shape:\r\n\r\n- **Point access:** `get`, `set`, `delete`.\r\n- **Bulk restore:** `save`, `load`, `remove`, `clear`.\r\n\r\nEvery shape obeys:\r\n\r\n- The stored value carries its own id; do not pass a separate id to `set`/`save`.\r\n- Every primitive is async and returns a `Promise`.\r\n- Deleting/removing a missing key is a no-op.\r\n- Concrete stores live in `stores/`; the interface stays in module-root `types.ts`; `create*Store` factories stay in `factories.ts`.\r\n\r\n## Barrel exports\r\n\r\n- `*/index.ts` is the sole public barrel.\r\n- Only an `index.ts` may re-export.\r\n- A barrel contains only `export * from './module.js'` declarations. Do not use\r\n  named, default, namespace, or type-only barrel exports.\r\n- A star-export collision is a design failure: rename the conflicting concept\r\n  at its owner and update consumers. Never hide the collision with a selective\r\n  barrel row.\r\n- Never re-export a symbol originating in another package; fix consumer imports to the originating package.\r\n- Implementation files export their own classes directly.\r\n- Expose every intentional top-level source export through its correct environment barrel.\r\n- Never let current consumer count gate later exposure. Developers receive the same supported\r\n  mechanisms the package uses, so they retain full control and customization.\r\n- If a declaration must not be public, make it a true local or runtime-private detail, or remove\r\n  the capability for a substantive reason. Never leave an intentional reusable export stranded\r\n  outside the barrel.\r\n- One-class-per-file evicts some classes from their only caller, and `export` on such a file is\r\n  structural rather than a statement of intent. Barrel that class when a consumer can construct it\r\n  from values they already hold. Intern it �?\" out of the barrel, and named in the package's parity\r\n  `INTERNAL` list �?\" when its constructor requires a value only its owner produces, or when the\r\n  public value is a projection of the instance rather than the instance. A class named in a public\r\n  signature is always barrelled.\r\n- Delete a barrel row whose class no consumer can construct, and delete its `@example` with it. A\r\n  row obliges a documented, runnable example, so a class kept public without one is drift that\r\n  parity cannot see.\r\n- When a symbol moves, update every import; never leave a compatibility re-export.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport * from './types.js'\r\nexport * from './constants.js'\r\nexport * from './errors.js'\r\nexport * from './validators.js'\r\nexport * from './helpers.js'\r\nexport * from './factories.js'\r\nexport * from './greeters/Greeter.js'\r\n```\r\n\r\n## System constraints\r\n\r\n- Build or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. Never write\r\n  `foo`, `bar`, or `baz`.\r\n- Expand an abbreviation the reader may not know on first use, with the short form in parentheses.\r\n  Skip the expansion for one this audience reads daily, such as `API`, `CLI`, `JSON`, `URL`, or a\r\n  file format.\r\n- Write a numeral for a technical quantity, a version, or a measurement. Write a date as\r\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` in evidence, commit messages, and reports. See `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing for what\r\n  separates a value from a count.\r\n- Use the serial comma. Mark omitted code with a comment in the sample's language, never with `...`.\r\n\r\n## Substitutions\r\n\r\nReplace each term in this table with its replacement. A literal code identifier is data, and so is\r\na sample string inside a code fence or a test fixture: quote each as itself, exempt from every row.\r\n\r\n| Term                     | Replacement                               |\r\n| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |\r\n| `should`                 | `must`, `can`, `might`, or the imperative |\r\n| `simply`, `easy`, `just` | Delete                                    |\r\n| `currently`, `now`       | Delete, or give the date                  |\r\n| `new`, `latest`          | Delete, or give the version               |\r\n| `utilize`, `leverage`    | `use`                                     |\r\n| `via`                    | `through`, `by using`                     |\r\n| `in order to`            | `to`                                      |\r\n| `e.g.`, `i.e.`           | `for example`, `that is`                  |\r\n| `etc.`                   | Bound the list, or recast the sentence    |\r\n| `performant`, `robust`   | The measured property                     |\r\n| `allows you to`          | `lets you`                                |\r\n| `and/or`                 | `and`, `or`, or `both`                    |\r\n| `since` (causal)         | `because`                                 |\r\n| `once` (temporal)        | `after`                                   |\r\n| `please`                 | Delete                                    |\r\n| `sanity check`           | `quick check`                             |\r\n| `dummy`                  | `placeholder`                             |\r\n| `blacklist`, `whitelist` | `denylist`, `allowlist`                   |\r\n| `master`, `slave`        | `primary`, `replica`                      |\r\n\r\n- `policy/no-banned-term` reads every comment and the prose sweep in `tests/setupPolicy.ts` reads\r\n  every authored Markdown file, and each matches the rows this table bans unconditionally. The rule\r\n  and the sweep leave `now`, `new`, `latest`, `once`, `since`, and `master` unmatched because those\r\n  rows carry a permitted sense, so rule a hit in one of those rows yourself.\r\n- Sweep case-insensitively and across inflections when checking prose against the preceding table. A\r\n  pattern for `easy` reaches neither `Easy` nor `easier`, and a temporal `once` most often appears\r\n  as a sentence-initial `Once`.\r\n- Rule every hit by the sense its row bans, not by the match. `once` counts as often as it means\r\n  `after`, and `new` names a value as often as it dates one. Record a hit in a permitted sense as\r\n  permitted rather than dropping it.\r\n- Name the pattern and the paths behind every sweep result, including a clean one. A result naming\r\n  neither reports on the population its pattern admitted rather than on the population it was drawn\r\n  from.\r\n- Write singular `they` for a person of unstated gender.\r\n- This table carries no row for `execute`, `abort`, `kill`, `terminate`, or `run`:\r\n  `.claude/rules/names.md` A\u0015 Fixed lifecycle vocabulary owns those words, and no row here overrides\r\n  it.\r\n\r\n## Not adopted\r\n\r\n- Do not add conversational personality, warmth, or memorability to a reply; the prose stays plain.\r\n- Do not import a license to break these rules where a departure reads better; an exception exists\r\n  only where a rule states it.\r\n- `We recommend` as a recommendation form �?\" refused in A\u0015 Voice and actor.\r\n- Do not apply a source guide's sample indentation or line-length chrome; the repository formatter\r\n  and `.claude/rules/typescript.md` decide code presentation.\r\n- Do not write an unspaced em dash; this project spaces it.\r\n- Do not cap a sentence at a word count; `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing owns sentence length.\r\n- Do not treat an external word list as vocabulary law; the preceding substitution table is the\r\n  adopted set.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - '**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts,vue}'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# TypeScript rules\r\n\r\nThe non-negotiables and design laws in `AGENTS.md` apply without exception and are not restated here. This file adds only what TypeScript itself decides.\r\n\r\n## Syntax and imports\r\n\r\n- Tabs for indentation.\r\n- No semicolons unless ASI requires one.\r\n- Single quotes.\r\n- Named exports only, except framework-required defaults.\r\n- ESM imports use explicit `.js` extensions for local TypeScript modules.\r\n- Place `import type` declarations before value imports.\r\n- Do not place blank lines between consecutive imports of the same kind.\r\n- Narrow an accepted `unknown` with a total guard rather than a conditional access.\r\n- A parameter property is a constructor parameter carrying any accessibility or `readonly` modifier.\r\n  The ban reaches every form, `readonly` alone included; declare the `#` field and assign it in the\r\n  constructor body.\r\n\r\n## Types\r\n\r\n- Put every reusable or public interface/type alias in the nearest authoritative `*/types.ts`.\r\n- Public collection properties and return types use `readonly T[]`, `ReadonlyMap<K, V>`, or `ReadonlySet<T>`.\r\n- Optional state is `T | undefined`; an optional lookup failure returns `undefined`.\r\n- `as const` annotates a literal with its own type and never overrides the checker, so the assertion\r\n  ban does not reach it. Use it to derive a literal union from a value and to fix a tuple's arity and\r\n  element types. Do not write it on a value whose contract is already declared; annotate the\r\n  declaration instead. A class field that holds one literal keeps `as const`\r\n  (`readonly code = 'ABORT' as const`): the vendored lint gate's `prefer-as-const` rule refuses the\r\n  annotated form, and a field whose type is a union of literals is annotated as the preceding\r\n  sentence states.\r\n\r\n## Immutability\r\n\r\n- Never mutate caller-owned inputs.\r\n- Use copy-on-write for internal state.\r\n- Return copies or readonly views; never leak a mutable internal reference.\r\n- Compute derived facts instead of persisting duplicate state.\r\n\r\n## Errors and outcomes\r\n\r\n| Condition                            | Required strategy                           |\r\n| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Programmer error or invalid argument | Throw an `AppError`                         |\r\n| I/O/network/external operation       | Return `Result<T, E>` or throw consistently |\r\n| Optional missing lookup              | Return `undefined`                          |\r\n| Invalid input inside a guard         | Return `false`; never throw                 |\r\n\r\nUse the existing outcome contract from a declared dependency or the owning environment; do not redeclare it at each call site. When no such contract exists and adding a dependency is not authorized, define this once in the owning `types.ts`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface Success<T> {\r\n\treadonly success: true\r\n\treadonly value: T\r\n}\r\n\r\ninterface Failure<E> {\r\n\treadonly success: false\r\n\treadonly error: E\r\n}\r\n\r\ntype Result<T, E = Error> = Success<T> | Failure<E>\r\n```\r\n\r\n- When `@orkestrel/contract` is declared, inspect and use its exact installed `Result`, construction/narrowing helpers, and `attempt` behavior instead of duplicating them.\r\n- Otherwise construct and narrow through the owning environment's centralized helpers.\r\n- Error classes expose a machine-readable `code` and optional `context`.\r\n- Every public error class ships with a guard such as `isAppError` for safe `catch` narrowing.\r\n\r\n## Comments and API documentation\r\n\r\n- Comments explain why, never restate what self-explanatory code does. The Writing rules in `AGENTS.md` and `.claude/rules/writing.md` govern their prose.\r\n- Every public export has complete TSDoc: description, `@param`, `@returns`, and `@example` where applicable.\r\n- The first sentence states what the symbol does in the third person with an `-s` verb �?\" `Creates`,\r\n  `Returns`, `Checks whether` �?\" and never repeats the symbol's name.\r\n- `policy/no-malformed-summary` reads that first sentence over every doc block a top-level export\r\n  declaration follows, and refuses an opening word that is not a third-person `-s` verb and a\r\n  sentence naming the declared symbol. A word ending in `s` that the rule's stop set does not name\r\n  passes whether or not it is a verb �?\" a plural noun such as `Files` included �?\" so read the sentence\r\n  in review as well.\r\n- Describe a boolean parameter as \"If `true`, �?�; if `false`, �?�\", and a boolean return as\r\n  \"True if �?�; false otherwise\".\r\n- Write a default as \"Default: �?�\" and a thrown error as \"Thrown when �?�\".\r\n- State a prerequisite and the failure behavior wherever the symbol has either.\r\n- `@deprecated` names the replacement first, then the reason.\r\n- Document an options object as one `@param`; describe its short fields under `@remarks`.\r\n- Private methods and overload-specific notes use single-line `//` comments, not public TSDoc.\r\n- Do not document speculative future product behavior unless requested.\r\n- Never use `@internal` to hide a method; make it `#` private.\r\n# Reconciling a round and ruling on it\r\n\r\nThe auditors return. Neither accepts; the orchestrator does. This is where a round becomes a\r\ndecision, and it is not delegable.\r\n\r\n## Reproduce before you act\r\n\r\nThe rule beneath this whole section: **run it rather than argue it.** Every judgement below is\r\ncheap once the probe exists and unreliable until it does.\r\n\r\nAn auditor's finding is a **hypothesis** until the orchestrator has run it. Reproduce every sharp\r\nclaim by hand, against the built output, before it enters a fix brief.\r\n\r\n**Build the hostile input outside the `try`.** A probe that wraps construction and invocation in one\r\ncatch cannot distinguish _the subject threw_ from _my harness threw_ �?\" a missing import, a wrong\r\narity, a `require` in an ESM context all surface as the finding you were hoping to see. Construct\r\nfirst, let harness failures crash loudly, and only guard the call under test.\r\n\r\nReproduction produces these outcomes, and all of them matter:\r\n\r\n- the finding **confirms** and is often **wider** than reported �?\" the reproduction reaches doors the\r\n  auditor did not try;\r\n- the finding **confirms but is bounded smaller** �?\" real, and not where the auditor thought;\r\n- the finding **evaporates**, because the auditor's input could not exercise what it claimed to test.\r\n\r\nSeparate a dead finding from a dead vector before evaporating anything. A reported vector the\r\ncompiler rejects refutes the vector alone; re-derive one the types admit, and record which vector\r\nwas actually tested.\r\n\r\nThe same reproduction discipline applies to your own probes. A probe whose input cannot reach the\r\ncode under test reports a pass that means nothing, and it will read exactly like a real pass.\r\n\r\n## A disagreement is rarely a tie\r\n\r\nWhen auditors return opposite verdicts on one claim, do not average them and do not prefer the\r\nengine you trust more. **Reproduce first** �?\" running the disagreement settles most of them outright,\r\nand it is the only method that can also find what neither auditor saw. Then find the question each\r\none answered. The common shapes:\r\n\r\n- **Both right about different objects.** One tested a case the other did not construct. This is a\r\n  `SPLIT-CLAIM`: the claim was a universal that carried more than one subject. It is **not** a\r\n  further verdict value �?\" one falsifying input makes a universal claim `BROKEN`, and succeeding on a\r\n  different object does not undo that. Split it, keep the original `BROKEN` if any subclaim is\r\n  broken, and carry the split into the successor brief.\r\n- **Both right about different halves of one claim number.** Same resolution: the claim number was\r\n  carrying more than one claim. Split and renumber.\r\n- **One right on the mechanism, the other on the criterion.** Take both. The reconciled ruling is\r\n  frequently neither proposal, and better than either, because each supplied a constraint the other\r\n  violated.\r\n- **The consequence disproves the premise.** An argument that an input class is unreachable is\r\n  answered by running it and showing what the reachable consequence is.\r\n\r\nRecord which engine was right and on what. A round whose disagreements are smoothed over teaches\r\nnothing to the next one.\r\n\r\n## Evidence custody\r\n\r\nBlind reports are immutable, and their independence is a property of the record, not of anyone's\r\nmemory. A reader six months out must be able to tell an unbiased blind verdict from one produced\r\nafter an auditor saw its counterpart's evidence �?\" otherwise the whole value of running blind is\r\nunverifiable after the fact.\r\n\r\nThese rules are enforceable:\r\n\r\n1. **A returned verdict is never edited** �?\" not by the auditor, not by the orchestrator.\r\n2. **Anything an auditor says after seeing another's report is a separate file beside that verdict**,\r\n   under `.orkestrel/<package>/`, named for the unit and the exposure, recording what was shown and\r\n   to whom. It is a durable record, not a journal, so it survives the campaign sweep.\r\n\r\nThat exchange is a **fallback, not a phase.** Reproduction comes first and settles most\r\ndisagreements. Reach for an exchange only when a specific factual question survives reproduction,\r\nscope it to that question, initiate it yourself, and run it once �?\" a second exchange is negotiation.\r\n\r\n**Ask the auditor to attack the other's evidence on that question. Never ask it to resolve the\r\ndisagreement, reconsider its position, or say whether the other changed its mind.** Constraining\r\nwhen, who, scope and frequency does nothing about convergence if the instruction itself invites it,\r\nand \"does their evidence change your claim?\" is the convergence prompt in its purest form.\r\n\r\n## Bound the finding\r\n\r\nState what is **not** broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks identical is correct. A\r\nfinding without a boundary is an alarm, and alarms get discounted wholesale �?\" including the true\r\nones next to them.\r\n\r\nThese boundaries earn their keep:\r\n\r\n- **Credit what the round got right.** If the hostile inputs adjacent to the hole are correctly\r\n  contained, say so and list them. It sharpens the finding to a point instead of an area.\r\n- **Show where the same-looking answer is correct.** When several exports answer a hostile input the\r\n  same way and only one is wrong, name what makes the difference �?\" usually that the correct ones\r\n  agree with a documented view, and the wrong one reads on an axis it then ignores.\r\n\r\n## Bound the fix before briefing it\r\n\r\nEstablish what over-correcting would break, and put it in the fix brief as a constraint. Both ends\r\nare usually wrong:\r\n\r\n- **too little** �?\" a patch to the one function, leaving the package holding conflicting standards\r\n  for the same thing, which is the inconsistency that produced the finding;\r\n- **too much** �?\" adopting the strictest sibling's rule verbatim, breaking a legitimate caller\r\n  pattern, and tripping \"no refusal was widened into a regression\" in the next round.\r\n\r\nFind the rule that fits both. It is usually about **agreement** rather than about categories �?\" what\r\na reader reads, its answer must carry �?\" and it dissolves the special cases rather than enumerating\r\nthem.\r\n\r\nMeasure a proposed fix before adopting it; it is itself a claim. Run it against the set it must\r\nnot break, including every case an earlier round pinned. Where it fails that set, document the\r\nlimit on the helper that owns it and pin the limit with a test that names it as one. A heuristic\r\nthat trades one wrong answer for another fails quietly; a stated boundary does not.\r\n\r\nWhere the choice is genuinely open, it is a design judgement with a subjective and an objective\r\nhalf, and it goes to a blind design pass before code. Ruling it unilaterally is how a fix round\r\nbecomes the next audit's finding.\r\n\r\n## Certifying an instrument\r\n\r\nThe control-population law in `.claude/rules/quality.md` binds here without restatement. What it\r\nleaves this round is the procedure.\r\n\r\nWhen a round certifies an instrument �?\" a pin, an identity check, a generated sweep �?\" the controls\r\nare usually drawn from whatever the instrument obviously covers, because that is where the examples\r\ntake the least construction. That sampling proves discrimination _within_ the population and is\r\nroutinely reported as proof the instrument works.\r\n\r\nSo before running controls, write down the instrument's **membership rule** in one sentence, then\r\nask what the rule excludes. Draw at least one control from there. These shapes have already cost a\r\nround each:\r\n\r\n- an AST comparison whose controls were all drawn from the literal classes present in the bodies it\r\n  guarded, blind to the classes absent from them;\r\n- a call-closure pin whose controls were all body-reachable functions, green for a function reached\r\n  only through a parameter default.\r\n\r\nThen write the sentences that matter: what the controls established, and what they did not. What\r\nthey did not establish is the one that gets skipped, and skipping it is how an instrument's\r\ncredibility outruns its evidence.\r\n\r\n## Ruling\r\n\r\n- Every retained finding names the fix-brief item that carries it. A finding with no carrier is a\r\n  dropped finding; walk the list once and check.\r\n- Drop, **on the record**, anything neither auditor can substantiate against the evidence.\r\n- Promote anything that must outlive the round into a durable artifact before the working files are\r\n  swept. What lives only in a scratch file did not survive.\r\n- The fix round's auditor is an engine that did not write it.\r\n- The next round's brief is this round's successor.\r\n\r\n## The threshold\r\n\r\nAccept when the brief's claims are **satisfied on evidence** �?\" the `PASS` terminal line the skill\r\ndefines, against a claim set that covers what the subject owns. Not green gates.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is the process working. A round that finds nothing because nobody tried\r\nis the failure; a round re-run because an attack can still be imagined never ends. Bound the claim\r\nset at the brief, rule on what it returned, and close.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

`RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE`    | const | `string` | Names the public message for an unexpected upstream relay failure �?\" `'relay provider failed'`, the fixed text every `error` frame carries, so an upstream failure's own message never reaches the browser.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |\r\n+ 14. **Pull and push observation surfaces on the `Agent`; the rest event-free.** The provider contract, the tool registry, the conversation store, and the `AgentContext` itself carry no Emitter, no `EventMap`, no `on` hook �?\" they stay purely functional (of the managers the context composes, the `InstructionManager` and `ScopeManager` carry their own `emitter`s and each `Conversation` owns one, while the `ToolManager`, `WorkspaceManager`, and `ConversationManager` are event-free �?\" as is the context that composes them). The `Agent` itself carries both a pull and a push surface. Pull: the `AgentChunk` stream (`stream().events`) yields per-token answer deltas, per-think reasoning deltas, usage chunks, and tool chunks for a live consumer. Push: the `emitter` (`AgentEventMap`) �?\" `start` (run begins), `turn` (each iteration), `tool` (a dispatched call + result), `usage` (a turn's usage), `deny` (an authority denial �?\" not in the chunk stream), `finish` (the settled result), `error` (a genuine failure), `abort` (a cancel), `exhaust` (a limit exhausted with unresolved tool intent �?\" fires instead of `abort`), and `fault` (a non-fatal automatic-compaction summarizer throw �?\" the run continues; the automatic-compaction clause) �?\" wired through the emitter pattern (`AgentOptions.on` hooks, the `AgentOptions.error` listener-error handler, a `readonly emitter`, `new Emitter({ on: options?.on, error: options?.error })`). Per-token / per-thinking deltas are the stream's job exclusively �?\" there is deliberately no `token` or `think` event. The emitter isolates a listener throw (it can never escape into the settle-once / wake-park loop) and routes it to its own `error` handler (the `error` option, surfaced as `(error, event)`, not a domain event; itself guarded against re-entrancy), so observation is provably side-effect-free on the 3A--hardened loop �?\" and every emit sits after the relevant state transition / settle, so it cannot reorder control flow. A cancelled run emits `abort` (the cancel reason) then `finish` (the settled partial); a genuine error emits `error` instead of `finish`. The loop's deterministic logic is pinned in the `src:core` mirror with a scripted `ProviderInterface`.\r\n+ **A `think: true` run needs headroom for reasoning.** Live reasoning deltas (`ProviderDelta` `'thinking'`) are not metered mid-stream (only `'content'` deltas are �?\" thinking is charged, like content, solely through the post-turn reconcile), so a thinking model can spend a large share of a tight budget's ceiling on its reasoning before any answer content streams �?\" the mid-stream trip can land while the model is still reasoning, committing an empty (or near-empty) `content` alongside `partial: true`. Give a `think: true` run enough budget headroom to cover its reasoning, not only its expected answer length.\r\n+ \t// expected �?\" this registry has no `schedulers` pool\r\n\u001b[2m Test Files \u001b[22m \u001b[1m\u001b[31m1 failed\u001b[39m\u001b[22m\u001b[90m (1)\u001b[39m\r\n\u001b[2m      Tests \u001b[22m \u001b[1m\u001b[31m3 failed\u001b[39m\u001b[22m\u001b[2m | \u001b[22m\u001b[1m\u001b[32m40 passed\u001b[39m\u001b[22m\u001b[90m (43)\u001b[39m\r\nexit: 1\r\nstatus after restore: []\r\n--- npm run test:guides restored\r\n\u001b[2m Test Files \u001b[22m \u001b[1m\u001b[32m1 passed\u001b[39m\u001b[22m\u001b[90m (1)\u001b[39m\r\n\u001b[2m      Tests \u001b[22m \u001b[1m\u001b[32m43 passed\u001b[39m\u001b[22m\u001b[90m (43)\u001b[39m\r\nexit: 0\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

src/server/helpers.ts\r\n/**\r\n* Builds a fetch-standard `Request` from a `node:http` `IncomingMessage` �?\" the\r\n* server-adapter half of the fetch/node conversion seam.\r\n*\r\n* @remarks\r\n* - `method` is carried over verbatim (defaulting to `GET` when absent).\r\n* - The URL is built against `options.origin` when given, otherwise a scheme\r\n*   derived from the connection (`https` when {@link isEncryptedSocket}, else\r\n*   `http`) plus the `Host` header (absent `Host` ��' `localhost`).\r\n* - Every request header is copied; multi-value headers are joined per fetch\r\n*   semantics (`', '`-joined), except `set-cookie`, whose values are each\r\n*   appended individually (fetch `Headers` preserves multiple `set-cookie`\r\n*   entries distinctly).\r\n* - For a method that carries a body (anything but `GET`/`HEAD`), the message\r\n*   is pumped chunk by chunk into a DOM-compatible `ReadableStream<Uint8Array>`\r\n*   (reconciling the DOM + node type worlds under the root config), with\r\n*   `duplex: 'half'` set as Node's fetch implementation requires for a\r\n*   streamed request body.\r\n* - A fresh `@orkestrel/abort` handle backs `request.signal`. It aborts when\r\n*   the request connection closes before the message finished\r\n*   (`!message.complete`), or when the paired `options.response` closes before\r\n*   its response finished (`!response.writableEnded`). A handler awaiting the\r\n*   signal therefore observes either side of a client disconnect the\r\n*   fetch-standard way, with zero router-specific API.\r\n*\r\n* @param message - The raw `node:http` request\r\n* @param options - Optional `origin` override and paired `response` for\r\n*   response-side disconnect tracking ({@link RequestOptions})\r\n* @returns A fetch `Request` whose `signal` fires on an incomplete request, or\r\n*   on a response-side client disconnect when `options.response` is provided\r\n*\r\n* @example\r\n* ```ts\r\n* import { buildRequest } from '@src/server'\r\n* import http from 'node:http'\r\n*\r\n* const server = http.createServer((incoming, response) => {\r\n* \tconst request = buildRequest(incoming, { response })\r\n* \tconsole.log(request.method, request.url)\r\n* })\r\n* ```\r\n*/\r\nfunction buildRequest(message, options) {\r\n\tconst method = message.method ?? When a write reports backpressure, the\r\n* body pump waits for `drain` before pulling again, unless the target closes,\r\n* errors, or is destroyed first. A `null` body ends `target` immediately with\r\n* no further writes. Total error posture: if `target` is destroyed mid-stream\r\n* (the client disconnected), the write loop stops and `target` is left as-is\r\n* rather than throwing an unhandled rejection �?\" a destroyed target is not\r\n* this function's error to surface.\r\n*\r\n* @param response - The fetch `Response` to write\r\n* @param target - The `node:http` response to write it to\r\n* @returns A A reusable regex, parser, header flattener, signer, or similar fragment has one implementation used by source, tests, and fixtures.\r\n- A would-be helper has these outcomes:\r\n  - trivial and genuinely single-use �+' fold into its caller;\r\n  - non-trivial or reusable �+' extract, export, unit-test, and route every duplicate through it.\r\n- `factories.ts`, `compilers.ts`, and `parsers.ts` are centralized files, not hiding places. Factory glue extracts to `helpers.ts`; pure compiler/parser recursion remains exported in its own kind file.\r\n- Every exported function in `parsers.ts` is named `parse*`. Every exported function in\r\n  `factories.ts` is named `create*`.\r\n- Those name forms are one-directional. A name does not place a function: `createWriteDirectory`\r\n  creates a directory rather than an entity and `isVacant` is a predicate rather than a `Guard<T>`,\r\n  so both stay in `helpers.ts`. Placement follows what the function is; the name form follows\r\n  placement.\r\n- Repair a violation of those forms by deciding what the function **is** first, then moving or\r\n  renaming to match. Both repairs exist and picking the wrong one does real damage.\r\n  - Wrong file, right name �+' **move it**. A `scan*` in `parsers.ts` is a pure lexical leaf that\r\n    belongs in `helpers.ts`. A function\r\n    returning a live entity is an entity factory and belongs in `factories.ts` whatever it is\r\n    called, so `restoreThing` there is misnamed, not misplaced. Renaming moves the published\r\n    surface and earns a version bump; that cost is the correct one to pay, and it is smaller than\r\n    the alternative.\r\n  - Never let the name choose. The implementation does not enter the graceful drain wait (`agent/node_modules/@orkestrel/server/dist/src/server/index.js:1897`).\r\n\r\nDo not place an unconditional `await server.stop()` before `destroy()` in failure cleanup. The server guide describes that reader as lazy and cached (`scaffold/guides/server.md:331`).\r\n\r\nThe implementation supports the narrower interpretation: server request setup creates a body-reading closure, and only invocation of that closure calls `readBody` (`agent/node_modules/@orkestrel/server/dist/src/server/index.js:1940`, `:2044`). The relay instead reads `request.body` through its own `readText` call (`agent/src/core/factories.ts:120`). With the fence�?Ts empty middleware chain, the inspected path does not apply the server�?Ts body cap.\r\n\r\nTimeouts remain separate. The server maps the configured request timeout to Node�?Ts `requestTimeout` (`agent/node_modules/@orkestrel/server/dist/src/server/index.js:1869`; `scaffold/guides/server.md:377`). The declaration does not establish how every interrupted upload manifests at the relay.\r\n\r\n**Settling probe:** use a small relay limit, a complete valid body at that limit, and the same body with a larger relay budget. Record authorization entry, handler response status, upstream entry, and client status. As a discriminating control, set a smaller server limit and compare direct body reading with middleware that actually invokes `context.body()`. This distinguishes server collection from relay collection. Do not attribute a client `413` from status alone.\r\n\r\n**Unknown 2 �?\" cancellation through a socket.**\r\n\r\nThe adapter builds a request with the outgoing response supplied, then links its signal to the server stop signal (`agent/node_modules/@orkestrel/server/dist/src/server/index.js:1919`, `:1928`). The router aborts on response close before `writableEnded` (`agent/node_modules/@orkestrel/router/dist/src/server/index.js:89`). The router guide describes this boundary at `scaffold/guides/router.md:631`.\r\n\r\nThe checked-in live Ollama case at `ollama/tests/service/relay.test.ts:76` asserts the local abort partial and recorded request fields. It does **not** explicitly await upstream signal abortion. That stronger assertion appears in `ollama/tests/src/core/integration.test.ts:159`, using a response that stays open.\r\n\r\n**Settling probe:** deliver a delta, hold the provider open on an explicit gate, confirm its signal remains unaborted, abort the browser operation, and await the upstream abort before shutdown. Observe provider finalization separately. Then call `stop()` and inspect the drain event. A finite script can finish while its bytes remain buffered, so it is an inadequate cancellation instrument.\r\n\r\n**Unknown 3 �?\" process signal registration.**\r\n\r\nThe process object inherits `EventEmitter` (`agent/node_modules/@types/node/process.d.ts:738`). Its listener API appends registrations without deduplication, and listener removal requires the registered function (`agent/node_modules/@types/node/events.d.ts:193`, `:340`). Copying the anonymous signal callback into repeated tests therefore leaves process-owned registrations unless explicitly removed.\r\n\r\nThe inspected declarations do not settle whether a signal registration keeps this host�?Ts worker alive. Do not promote that assumption into a fact.\r\n\r\n**Settling probe:** in an isolated host process and the configured Vitest worker, register and remove the listener, observe registrations, and check natural exit after disposal. An intentionally open listener socket supplies the liveness control. Regardless of the result, substitute awaited test teardown: signal registration does not provide assertion-failure cleanup.\r\n\r\n## Refusals\r\n\r\nThe proposal excludes these changes and claims.\r\n\r\n- No dependency installation, package edits, runtime source edits, vendored-file edits, or Ollama edits.\r\n- No fence edits that would require changing the off-limits titled examples.\r\n- No all-interface test listener, fixed port, or IPv6-only binding.\r\n- No claim that default-server-limit and default-relay-limit status alone identifies the refusing component; the defaults coincide at `1_048_576` bytes.\r\n- No claim that shutdown-induced upstream abortion proves client cancellation.\r\n- No claim that passing prose guards proves execution.\r\n- No browser validation claim from the Node project.\r\n\r\nThe general no-network default does not foreclose the objective: the user expressly requests a loopback HTTP hop, and the test rules prescribe loopback fixture-server binding.\r\n\r\n## Measurements\r\n\r\nThe brief supplies an installation receipt with exit code `0`, router version `0.0.14`, and server version `0.0.19`. Verbatim versus substituted\r\n\r\nExecuted verbatim, in the fence's own order and wording: the `createRelay({ provider, authorize })` call with its bearer comparison; `createDispatcher({ routes: [{ method: 'POST', path: '/relay', handler }] })`; `createServer({ dispatcher, state: () => undefined })`; `await server.start()`; `dispatcher.handle(request, undefined)` as the body of the `serve` entry; `server.stop()`.\r\n\r\nSubstituted, each named in the prose at line 1097:\r\n\r\n| Fence text | Transcription | Reason |\r\n| --- | --- | --- |\r\n| `parser: createNDJSONParser` | `parser: createParser` | Already substituted. `agent/tests/setup.ts:198` throws on a malformed line where the published parser skips it. |\r\n| `createServer({ dispatcher, state })` | adds `host: '127.0.0.1'` | `.claude/rules/tests.md:31` fixes a fixture listener to `127.0.0.1` on an ephemeral port.
